Agentview_high is sufficient for identity; sample body/top pixels away from silhouette edges and median xy. The wrist view from above the agentview anchor should show the same dark bottle under the gripper; reject wrist geometry if it jumps to the bowl or cabinet.\n\nPlate: look for the white ceramic disc with red concentric rings. It can be confused with the gray stove burner because both are circular and flat in depth, so classify by RGB before using back_project. Sample the white inner region and ring-balanced pixels, not the rim alone; use the perceived center as a target, then bias a few cm deeper if the bottle hangs toward the near edge. Absolute coordinates from this run are counter-examples only and must not be cached.\n\n## Fragility flags\nThe most fragile step is the low release: a correct bottle grasp and a visually plausible placement may still not terminate if the bottle is on the plate rim/edge. Fallback is a short closed-gripper contact nudge at the same low z followed by a second release.\n\n## Difficulty and reliability\nConverged in 1 attempt with one in-episode recovery nudge. Expected single-shot rate is good when the grasp is visually confirmed and the release is followed by a low centering check. Residual risk: bottle hanging offset varies by grasp, so future runs should inspect the plate contact before deciding whether to nudge.\n\n## Cross-refs\n[[can-pick-visual-confirmation]]\n"

Bowl region center_xyz (-0.096, -0.0084) is the midpoint of world xy over the window — differs from median (-0.123). Window may include some pixels beyond the bowl. Let me refine: my region [540,600]x[455,550] — the bowl appears centered around col 500, row 570. Midpoint (row 570, col 502). From earlier single-pixel: (570,500) → (-0.1284,-0.0102). So bowl center ≈ (-0.128, -0.010), radius ~0.06.

Hmm, midpoint of region gives -0.096 which suggests the window extends beyond bowl on the near side (larger x). Since bowl spans cols ~440-565, rows ~525-615, center px ≈ (570, 502). x from back_project of (570,500) = -0.1284. y=-0.0102.

Actually careful: within the bowl interior, z of the interior floor is lower and the far side wall is occluded; back projection of the interior bottom gives the actual geometric interior center if it's the floor. Our sample at (570,500) z=0.9116 — that's likely the interior bottom center. Good: bowl center ≈ (-0.128, -0.010).

Now, where's the bowl's rim height? Interior floor at 0.9116. Hmm but the plate is at 0.908 (top surface). If the bowl's interior floor is at 0.9116, that's ABOVE the plate top surface, which is impossible unless the bowl sits on the table and its interior floor is above the plate's top. Plate top surface z ~0.908 could reflect plate being thin (e.g., 0.01 thick on table at 0.898). Bowl interior floor at 0.9116 means bowl base bottom at maybe 0.905 and interior floor above? That doesn't work either — interior floor should be above the outer bottom by wall thickness ~0.005. So bowl bottom ~0.906, interior floor 0.9116 → matches table ~0.90? And plate: bottom on table 0.90, thickness 0.008 → top 0.908. Consistent! Table z ≈ 0.898-0.90.

So bowl rim: sampled pixel z 0.9355 at (540,480) — that might be upper inner wall. Rim likely ~0.945. Bowl height ~0.045.

For the place: bowl must be released with its bottom on the plate top (z=0.908). When grasped from over the rim, eef holds bowl with its bottom maybe ~7-10 cm below eef. Better: measure held offset by wrist after lift.

Approach plan:
1. move_to above bowl at (-0.128, -0.010, 1.15) open.
2. pi0_pick("pick up the bowl", max_chunks 10, lift_thresh 0.05, gripper_closed_thresh 0.06).
3. Inspect. set_gripper +1 for 8.
4. Measure held bowl: wrist image → segment bowl in wrist to get bowl center xy relative to eef. Compute offset. Then target eef = plate_center - offset.
5. Carry at z ~1.10-1.12 then descend to place, release.

Also the On predicate for bowl-on-plate: the bowl bottom needs to be on the plate. Since the bowl's diameter ~0.13 and plate diameter ~0.13, need a good centering.

Note: "Test placement targets by holding the object over them and watching libero_terminated" — On() may fire while held. But better to just place.

Potential issue: bowl is grasped by rim and Pi0 may swing it. Let's start.

First pre-position. Home eef z=1.179. Move above bowl at z=1.15.

Perhaps I should intercept: run pi0_pick with max_chunks 14 — the descent is Pi0's own. I can't control its depth. Alternatively, I could pre-grasp manually: descend the eef to just above the rim and close the gripper myself (set_gripper +1) — the "manual rim grasp". But a manual vertical grasp on the near rim: eef descending to rim level z~0.945 at the rim: fingers straddle the rim... but a top-down gripper closing horizontally at a rim would grip the rim wall between the fingers only if one finger is inside and the other outside. If the eef is at the bowl center, both fingers are inside the bowl (gripping air). Pi0 handles this by offsetting so one finger is inside and one outside. That's why memory says pre-position offset +0.045 in y.

Actually let's reconsider using this: pre-position the eef at bowl_y + 0.045 (so one finger inside the bowl, one outside the rim on the +y side), then descend and close manually. That would give a solid grasp on the rim's top edge (fingers pinching the rim from inside and outside). This is exactly the "rim grasp" that works reliably for mugs/bowls.

Wait, but the finger gap: the fingers are ~0.04 wide each. With the eef at y = bowl_y+0.045 and the bowl radius 0.065, the rim (+y side) is at y = bowl_y + 0.065. The fingers close along ±y? No — the gripper closes along its own axis, which by default is the y axis of the hand frame. The eef quaternion is roughly identity (x aligned with world x), so the fingers open along world ±y. So fingers at y = eef_y ± 0.0395 when open. With eef_y = bowl_y + 0.045: finger1 at y = bowl_y + 0.0045 (inside the bowl), finger2 at y = bowl_y + 0.0845 (outside the bowl, since the rim is at +0.065). So closing, finger1 and finger2 would meet the rim wall at y = bowl_y+0.065 between them — yes! finger1 pushes on the inside of the rim, finger2 on the outside. Excellent: a proper rim pinch at height z ≈ rim.

So: descend eef to z ≈ rim height (0.945) at (bowl_x, bowl_y + 0.045), then close with set_gripper +1. This is the "manual rim grasp". The grasp depth matters: the fingers must be at the rim level, not below it. The finger pads extend ~2-3cm below the eef link origin... Let's figure out from the previous data: Pi0 descended to eef z min 0.915 and grasped the bowl (gap 0.014). If the fingers straddle the wall at 0.014 gap... at that depth, the bowl outer radius is smaller.

Hmm, to manually grasp at the rim: the finger tips should be at the rim's z (0.945) ± 2cm. Since the fingers hang below the eef, I should command eef z ≈ 0.965-0.97 (so the fingertips are at ~0.945).

But risky: if the fingers close too high (above the rim), they grip air. If too low, they wedge the bowl's outer wall — the bowl would still be held (friction) but weaker.

Hmm. Given uncertainty, Pi0's own grasp did produce a clean lift; the slip was in transport. So let me retry Pi0 and focus on a gentler carry. That's the least risky. If it fails again, I'll try the manual rim grasp.

Actually — a smarter idea to shorten the transport dramatically: after picking, instead of a long horizontal carry, I can pre-grasp and then move in a two-stage with re-clamps. Let's just do 3 short hops with re-clamps and low carry height.

Run pi0_pick now.

Hmm, this matters. If grasping low on the wall (bowl bottom ~1cm below eef), the fingers are near the bowl's bottom. Descending until the bowl bottom touches the plate means the fingers touch the plate top too. Since the fingers are open at ±0.0065 gap... the closed fingers are inside/around the bowl wall, so they'd contact the plate via the bowl. It's just contact — OSC will stop or push.
